Madam President, I would like If I were to stand here and describe every announcement, I would well pass my written speaking time, and I will therefore try to keep to a small number of them. I would therefore also like to raise the issue of secret coercive measures. At present, secret coercive measures are granted against a specific telephone number or location. This means that criminals create a pattern in which they switch mobile phones or places where crime is committed or planned. As the possibility of obtaining a permit for new coercive measures is time-consuming, the work of the crime prevention authorities is hampered by the current legislation. By linking secret coercive measures to a person rather than a specific telephone number or place, this can be facilitated. After all, Sweden is in a situation where we have about one shooting a day and one shooting a week – most recently in a gym in the middle of the day in central Stockholm. Society should make use of all means at its disposal and create new conditions for halting this development, and police tools against criminals should be higher in line than the possible integrity of criminals. A large proportion of opposition parties are on the train and address an announcement to the government. Madam President, I would like As I have said, the opposition parties have a lot of announcements – too many to go through in a single speech, so I will go on to highlight proposals which the opposition unfortunately cannot hear here and now but which it is not impossible for us to agree on in the future. I will begin with the question of a reduced limit for compulsory detention. Organised crime is exacerbated by the rapid return of serious criminals from detention to their former circumstances. One measure that the Swedish Democrats, the Moderates and the Christian Democrats want to implement is to reduce the penalty value for acts that lead to mandatory detention from today's two years to one year. More criminals who commit serious crimes would thus remain in custody. This would not only improve the view of the judiciary, but also prevent the recurrence of crime by these individuals and make society more secure for all citizens. The freedom of its law-abiding citizens must weigh more heavily than that of criminals. Unfortunately, the other parties say no to this. Madam President, I would like The motion for a resolution also includes the proposal for enhanced witness protection, which has gathered broad political support across party boundaries. Unfortunately, only the Swedish Democrats, the Moderates and the Christian Democrats want to introduce a system of anonymous witnesses. The measure is being requested by both the police and the prosecutors and would undoubtedly have allowed more people than at present to testify, which is one of the most important measures in the fight for more gang criminals to be convicted. Threats against witnesses are common in gang environments, and several times threats have also been carried out. In 2018, for example, a 40-year-old man and his wife were killed after the man in an interrogation told the police what he had seen. The government is not doing enough to protect witnesses. If it is an important and concrete measure we are going to take to get more people to testify, it is the opportunity to testify anonymously. This is extremely important, but the government also says no to it. Madam President, I would like When the opposition is in the driver's seat for tightening up penalties, for legislative changes and for new tools to the police, the government sits in the gallery. Uncertainty is the great issue of internal freedom of our time. Crime must be combated, and society must be more secure. Today's development is not good enough. Unfortunately, it is a total disaster. The Social Democrats cannot explain in any way the 147% increase in the number of shootings since they took over government power almost eight years ago. In 2010, 9 people were shot to death here in Sweden in a whole year. The first quarter of this year, we have already passed twice, and further development will go far beyond the gloomy record of 47 shootings per year in 2020 and 2021. The Riksdag thus addresses eleven announcements to the Government. That is good, but much more needs to be done. Above all, Sweden needs a new government.

I will concentrate on a couple of announcements concerning the victim's perspective. Last year, one in three entrepreneurs in Sweden suffered from crime. These are worrying figures, to say the least, and unfortunately the figures have been pointing in one direction for several years – the wrong direction. There's no point in reporting a crime. It only takes a lot of time, and the matter will still be closed. Often it is written off directly, so-called direct write-off. Madam President, I would like Of course, every crime committed must be taken seriously by our law enforcement authorities, and any victim who is subjected must, of course, be listened to. It is not only reasonable but absolutely fundamental in the rule of law that no matter who the victim is and regardless of the type of crime committed, it should be investigated and hopefully also prosecuted. There's no point in filing a report. The police's discharge decision comes faster than a letter in the mail. Then it is of course justified to write off the case directly. However, when a case is dismissed directly despite the fact that a crime has occurred and despite the fact that there are suggestions to continue, confidence in the judiciary is undermined. Unfortunately, it was such a lack of confidence that I heard from the entrepreneurs I met the other week. In the long run, this is serious for our whole society. This is to enable more crimes to be investigated and cleared up, especially in cases where there is a clear interest in crime victims. Madam President, I would like I am happy to talk more about the position of victims and what we can do to strengthen them in a stressful situation, as it is almost always for those who have been victims of crime. In particular, I would like to say something about what we can do for those victims who, if the investigation by the police and prosecutors is solid and successful, will eventually end up in the courtroom. There are several measures that society can take to make the victim, who in the courtroom becomes a plaintiff, feel safe all the way. In most cases, a conviction in the district court does not mean the end of the proceedings for the victim of a crime. Many who are convicted in the district court choose to appeal, and then a new process awaits in the court of appeal – so also for the plaintiff. Even if the plaintiff rarely needs to undergo further hearings in the Court of Appeal, he may still need to be present there, and regardless of whether physical presence is required, the plaintiff is very much affected by the outcome of the Court of Appeal. The cost of public assistance has skyrocketed over the last decade, in principle without functioning control mechanisms. This is stated by the Swedish National Audit Office in a recent review. Instead of ensuring effective cost control, however, the government has chosen a seemingly easy way to reduce some of the costs, namely to significantly reduce the possibilities for the victim to be supported by a plaintiff in an infringement. Almost four years ago, rules came into force whereby a plaintiff's appointment should not automatically remain in breach. A plaintiff has an important role to play for the victim, who, in addition to dealing with a psychologically stressful situation, should understand and be able to defend his or her rights. This is particularly important in cases involving sexual offences, violence in close relationships and honor-related crime. But even in cases involving fraud offences, for example, the plaintiff can have a tough time, and it can be difficult to understand how damages can be claimed. The Government has commissioned the Swedish National Courts Administration to evaluate the amendment of the law that was implemented in 2018. But we in the Center Party want to press for this to happen urgently, and we want the government to take steps as soon as possible to strengthen the right of plaintiffs in violation. We appreciate the fact that all parties agree with us and have agreed to this announcement. Madam President, I would like Finally, I would like to say something about our special courts, namely the Land and the Environment Courts. These courts often get extremely complex and extensive cases on their table. Unfortunately, we have seen how the legal processes in such cases are slowing down in a completely unacceptable way. In these courts, there are often trials of permits relating to the possibility of carrying out or expanding activities that form part of our basic industry, such as mining or lime mining. Now, most recently in the case of Cementa, we have become aware of the enormous impact such court proceedings can have on how issues related to our basic industry should be dealt with. It is therefore extremely important that these special courts be given the resources they need so that they are able to deal effectively and legally with even extensive and complex cases within a reasonable timescale. Madam President, I would like The Christian Democrats want the judiciary to be given appropriate means of coercion to prosecute crimes and, of course, support the increased possibility of listening to persons suspected of serious crime. However, we believe that interception decisions should be linked to a person rather than to telephone numbers, addresses or certain electronic communications equipment. This assessment is also shared by the authorities, such as the police and the Customs Agency, which are supposed to actually use the legislation – they also advocate that the coercive measures be linked to the person rather than to the place. Criminal networks are often very aware of the limitations to police coercive measures and exploit these restrictions to the maximum. We Christian Democrats believe that the law should be designed in such a way that decisions on secret interception as well as other decisions on monitoring, such as secret data reading, should be linked to the person to be watched. It is gratifying that a majority in the committee shares this assessment and makes two announcements on this. Madam President, I would like One of the fundamental prerequisites for tackling the serious crime that operates in our country is that the judicial system can and must prosecute those who commit the crimes. We said that one example of this could be the introduction of a so-called training premium. Well, the daily allowance had been taken and reduced to a minimum to pay it instead after the military service had been completed as a training premium. So what the Ministry of Defence did was to impair the conditions for soldiers and conscripts. You can ask the question: Do you want your salary now at the end of the month, or do you want it in six months? Of course, everyone wants the money on their pocket so that you can pay their snuff and rent, but there the Armed Forces were creative in tricking the soldiers. Let me take another example. The conscript who, after completed military service, thrives so well that you choose to stay and start an aspirant course as an officer of the Swedish Armed Forces will lower your salary. Of course, it is not called salary, but the allowance you receive every day as a conscript at the end of your military service is higher than it is at the beginning of the aspirant service to become an officer. The Swedish Armed Forces thus reward our officers' aspirants in a first career step by lowering their salary. Is this a good staff policy? No. Although both we in the Moderates and the Officers' League have pointed this out to the government, it chooses not to do anything. So there is much that the government could do that it chooses not to do. While the government's goal in terms of standing units is missed year after year, it chooses not to look at the benefits or how more could be attracted to apply to become a periodically or continuously employed soldier. We can talk about this in terms of defence policy. Just as our Jas planes are our spearheads in the air, our standing units are our spearheads on the ground. When something happens, we need to quickly be out and face any threats that may exist. If we do not have standing units, we will be late or fail in what is the task of the Armed Forces. This is not discussed to any great extent, but the government chooses not to look at the problem. This is not just a problem in Sweden, it is a problem throughout Western Europe. The problem will not be any less so, but it will affect both our defence capabilities and our readiness for a coup. To summarise this, Madam President, the Swedish Armed Forces' ability to recruit and retain competent personnel will be crucial to its ability to grow and solve its tasks. i-G1J9zW7BoZ1FiL3VjahrsR	Madam President, I would like Since the rescheduling of defence policy in 2015 and the major economic additions, we see a defence power growing. Military and operational capabilities are built, developed and improved. Let me therefore, Madam President, point out some, what I mean, key aspects in this regard. A future defence will be based on universal and gender-neutral military service. A strong popular anchoring and a broad recruitment base is a prerequisite for the defence, where duty duty is a very special type of service. In line with the establishment of the reactivated military service, we see, for example, a sharp increase in the number of applicants for the officer programme. The obligation thus serves several functions, both to create defence will and joint responsibility and to recruit officers. The Swedish Armed Forces' annual report for 2021 shows that approximately 5,900 conscripts started basic education in 2021 and that the objective of 5,000 conscripts was thus exceeded. At a time when the defence is to grow, it is important to reach the entire recruitment base of the population in order to employ the most suitable and to provide adequate and efficient human resources. An equal organisation also contributes to the legitimacy, credibility and popular attachment of military defence. Progress is moving in the right direction, but unfortunately we still see a clearly lower interest among women than among men. Perhaps it is not surprising given that military service has previously been manly. For generations, our fathers and grandfathers have been called to contract, while for women it was not even possible to apply for military training until 1980 and then only in a few areas. It was only as late as 1989 that women were granted access to all positions and parts of the Armed Forces. Until 1994, women were required to apply for military service in order to qualify for military service. It is first with the reactivated conscription that women are called to conscription and conscription on the same terms as men. The annual report also states that the proportion of women who started basic education with compulsory military service in 2021 was 21 per cent, an increase of 3 per cent compared to the previous year and an increase of 5 per cent compared to 2019. The Swedish Armed Forces' goal is that the proportion of women should be at least 25 per cent. Secondly, the trend persists with increased student volumes of vocational and reserve officers' training, and the number of students who started the officers' programme in 2021 was the highest since the programme started in 2007. The number of professional officers also increased during 2021, due, among other things, to large inflows from basic officers' training, re-employment and to few professional officers choosing to leave the Swedish Armed Forces. At the same time, there is an imbalance in supply. The Swedish Armed Forces have a mandate to design the professional and reserve officers' supply to counter this imbalance. For this purpose, the Swedish Armed Forces have also taken several steps, including creating several ways into the profession through the special officer training. Thirdly, reserve officers, i.e. officers who have their main occupation outside the Swedish Armed Forces but can, if necessary, go in and solve tasks, are extremely important for our preparedness and for the Swedish Armed Forces to be able to carry out their duties and produce fully staffed military units. It is necessary for the government to take concrete measures to facilitate and strengthen the ability of reserve officers to serve, for example, so that they are entitled to leave of absence for service in the Swedish Armed Forces, just as the category GSS/T has. Fourthly, at the committee's proposal, the Parliament has decided to announce to the government that it should investigate the design of modern civil service. In accordance with the Total Defence Obligations Act, all three parts to increase the operational capacity to strengthen the defence will are important: military service, civil service and public service obligations. It is extremely important that the Swedish Armed Forces continue to work on developing conditions, forms of employment and other incentives. i-KVjv19k8QDxa21ZLQHcQCW	Madam President, I would like We can see that for years we have been talking about defence policies that we are in a troubled time and a very troubled world situation. It has become increasingly clear and evident in recent weeks. Russia's invasion of Ukraine has completely reversed the previous playing field. Old truths are no longer valid. Our way of life is threatened. To be equipped in the face of crises, both civilian and military, and extremely armed attacks are therefore of great importance in order to secure our democracy, our well-being and our way of life. The total defence decision 2021 – 2025 means a historic strengthening of the defence and a major expansion of the operations. It is a reinforcement that is really needed. We see the greatest investment in military defence since the Second World War. The aim is that at least 2% of our bnp should be dedicated to the defense. The military defence will continue to be strengthened and operational capabilities increased. This also applies to the ability to carry out operations with other countries and organisations. One of the goals is that during the period 2021 – 2025 and 2026 – 2030 the Swedish Armed Forces will gradually increase the ability to provide and receive military support to and from other states and organisations and together be able to conduct operations. The basic organization's growth has been initiated through the establishment of Norrland Dragoon Regiment, Älvsborg Amphibious Regiment, Upplands Flygflottilj and Dalregementet during the autumn of 2021. The Västernorrland Regiment was inaugurated in January 2022, and preparations are underway to re-establish the Bergslagen artillery regiment. In the light of the security policy developments in Sweden's immediate neighbourhood, the Minister of Defence decided on 8 February to consult the Defence Committee to strengthen the defence's ability in the short term. On 8 March, the Swedish Armed Forces presented proposals for measures to further strengthen the operational capability. Among other things, the Authority recommends that the short-term approach should continue to address the imbalances between impact and support functions and strengthen the support functions of intelligence and security services, management and logistics. The agency also calls for freedom of action to enable adaptation and optimisation, reduced decision-making times in several areas to accelerate the pace of growth and to give greater weight to the needs of total defence in a number of policy areas. In addition to suggestions on areas where capacity can be strengthened, the Authority's input also includes proposals for revising certain legislation and regulations. The direction is set. The pace is rising. Madam President, I would like Let me devote a few minutes to what is beyond logistics, weapon systems and infrastructure. If this is something that the Russian invasion of Ukraine has taught us, it is precisely this: joint responsibility, motivation and willingness to defend our country. My point, then, is that military capabilities are also being built beyond weapon systems, regiments and financial grants. Our common desire to defend Sweden is central. It's a matter for everyone. When the collective memory is erased, we have a central task to learn to understand and draw conclusions from building military capabilities. Madam President, I would like A lot of years ago, an acquaintance told me that he had helped his old mother move away from the house. It had become too big, and the lady had difficulty walking on the stairs. In the basement they had found 40 kilos of coffee, and my friend asked her mother what to do with all the coffee. Of course, the lady, born in the 20th century, replied: Not another war without coffee. The story itself is a bit funny, but it also contains a sting of seriousness. Take, for example, the pandemic crisis we have been living with since 2020. The crisis has clearly exposed the shortcomings of our well-being and the resilience of society. It is the result of a lack of preparedness storage, planning and recurrent exercises. It is at least as much a result of political and economic doctrines that have had a great impact on the organization of society. Decades of privatisation of social functions, ranging from rail to elderly care, have made society less resilient and more difficult to manage. Governance models, drawn from the manufacturing industry, have been applied to public goods without taking into account the social pressures of a crisis. The combination of weak civil preparedness and political doctrines has proved to be devastating to society's ability to protect us when the crisis strikes us. The lightening and fun story about the lady and the 40 kilos of coffee sets the light on a most topical question: What do we do when the crisis or war comes? For even in the event of crises or extremely armed attacks, society must function and democracy must be maintained. Society should work and welfare should be there. The key to the military capability we create through weapons systems, human resources policies and financial resources is therefore also access to civilian preparedness, peoples' support and defence will. They are interdependent and interdependent.
